**20 Oct 2026, evening: 0.0.99 (built and pushed, not yet tested): Obelisk puzzle solutions from walkthroughs and the script (Francesco asked for a walkthrough search).** Sources: Ludo.guide's Obelisk page (block sequences per floor and target moon: 32F 3 then 1 for 4/8; 52F 4/8 3-4-2-1-2, 8/8 1-3-4; 77F right 4/8 2-1-4-1-4, 8/8 1-2-2-3; 77F left 4/8 back and forth between 0 and 3, 8/8 3-1-3-1; a later route 8/8 2-3-2-1 or 4/8 1-2-1-2-1-2-3; 128F the Moirai, letters), Samurai Gamers' Obelisk puzzle guide (which route opens the chest or the path, by step count; its steps are images). The script (`f031.bf`, procedures `NNN_01eve_*`; `research/gamedata/f031/puzzle.txt`) gives the structure: a "Time passed quickly" tile is a bridge that moves the player to a block (`01pos_NN`) and advances the moon by that block's value (IX 249: 0 to 4; no IX 249 means 0); start bridges lead from the start to a block, other bridges join two blocks both ways; "Will you go back?" tiles lie between the start and the first blocks (on the way back: Francesco's "No" on 32F was a detour); exit tiles clear flag 1614. The guides' numbers are block values: matched to the bridges, they sum to 8 (8/8) or 12 (4/8, past full). Spheres: script areas 3 (`01eve_01`), 5 (`01eve_05`), 7 (`01eve_30` right, `01eve_50` left), 11 (`01eve_30`), 14 (`01eve_30`). **Tables (`TimePuzzle.Solutions`, keyed by script area and sphere):** 32F: `01eve_03`, `01eve_07`, exit `01eve_06` (the 0.0.98 table had the detour); 52F path (8/8): `01eve_07`, `01eve_10`, `01eve_11`, the nearer of exits `01eve_14` / `01eve_15`; 77F left path (8/8): `01eve_51`, `01eve_58`, `01eve_60` twice, the nearer of `01eve_55` / `01eve_56`. Waiting: 77F right (sphere 30: blocks 33 (0), 34 (2), 35 (1), 36 (3), 37 (4); bridges 31, 32, 33 from the start, 39 (34-33), 40 (35-34), 41 (36-33), 42 (37-35); exits 37, 38; the guides do not say which route opens the path), script area 11 (no exit tile with 1614: an end found otherwise), script area 14 (128F). The same tile can count twice (left out of every volume between). The nearer-exit choice is an assumption: to check in game (the chest exit may be the nearer). **To test (52F and 77F left):** touch the sphere; Page Up says only "Next step: Time passed quickly", step by step, then the exit; the path opens.

**20 Oct 2026, 15:31 session: 0.0.100 (built and pushed, not yet tested): flat volume boxes read with their 4 real corners; the Obelisk 52F bridge tiles listed.** Francesco on 52F (field 31 area 4, script area 5) with 0.0.99: touching the sphere (15:48:24, `[Nav:Puzzle] Time puzzle on ... 4 steps known`) gave "Next step: Story event, 4 metres, south-west. 1 of 1" (`01eve_07`); he walked it, the game moved him to block `01pos_09` (step 1 done, next `01eve_10`), and then the Pages list was the full one (six points, the S-Terminal door among them) with no next step. He took a "Will you go back?" tile, Yes, the puzzle went off, and he saved suspend data at the start (15:49:22). **Cause:** `01eve_10` to `01eve_15` (gEvnRng 8 to 13, read from `f031_005.f2`: `01eve_10, 14, 13, 11, 12, 15`) were never in the point list and no log line said why: a box with `sizez` 0 (a flat plane, crossed; the dump prints `size x y z`) has 4 real corners and 4 left at 0 0 0 by the game (every dump: 135 boxes with sizez 0, all padded; 1980 others, none). The mod read all 8 as corners, so the footprint reached the area's origin; the bridges lie 1750 to 2500 south of it, became wider than 1500 ("room-sized", the area-trigger rule) and were dropped before the script check. `01eve_06` to `09` (z 1350 and 1500, or boxes with depth) passed. The same padding pulled every flat box's centre halfway to the origin and its bottom to y 0 (`VolumeSpan`, `BoxBottom`, `WarpMap.ZoneOf`). **Fix:** `FieldData.BoxCorners` (4 when `sizez` is 0) in all four. Also: an event volume dropped as room-sized is now logged with the others left out ("room-sized, an area trigger"); when the puzzle's next step is not among the points, `[Nav:Puzzle] The next step, X, is not among this area's points` (the list then stays full, as before). **Checked on all dumps:** only event volumes have real flat boxes (no door, person or box); 22 events change verdict: the Obelisk's 30F bridges `01eve_06` and `01eve_07` (the 30F table's steps 2 and 3: they would have been missed too), area 1's `01eve_09`, and in other fields volumes that the dry run finds idle (field 22 area 9, field 23 area 2, the overworld field 4), one story event (Kabukicho Prison area 2 `01eve_03`, now listed) and one with no procedure (left out). The overworld's flat planes stay out (`!(world && sizez == 0)`): field 3's `01eve_07` to `14` set the encounter table (`fldCommand_FLD_SET_ENCOUNT`), and every flat plane of fields 2 to 6 was left out before. **Also seen:** the bridges between blocks (`005_01eve_10` to `13`) show no message: they move the player (`evtCommand_MODEL_MOVE`, 0x17) by the side entered (`fldCommand_DIR_HIT_CNTR`, 0x5F), add the block's value to IX 249 and run procedure 17 (`scrCommand_SCR_RUN`, 0xA5): the "silent tiles" of 0.0.97, now named by the logged command table; their point says "Next step: Story event" without a narration. The 52F script agrees with the table: `01eve_07` to block 09 (+1), `01eve_10` from 09 to 08 (+3), `01eve_11` from 08 to 10 (+4), exit `01eve_14` beside block 10 (8/8). **To test (52F, from the suspend data at the start):** touch the sphere; Page Up says only "Next step: ..." four times in turn (the first two "Story event", south-west then east, the third south, the last the exit), Home walks to each; the path opens. **20 Oct 2026, 16:08 session: 0.0.100 verified on 52F; 0.0.101 (built and pushed, not yet tested): quiet puzzle walks and a solved line.** Francesco retried 52F from his suspend data: sphere (16:09:34), then "Next step: Story event, 4 metres, south-west", "3 metres, east", "3 metres, south", "3 metres, south" (`01eve_07`, `01eve_10`, `01eve_11`, exit `01eve_14`, the nearer exit as assumed), each "1 of 1"; the puzzle went off at the exit (16:10:03) and the path opened (collision polygon 352 rose 400, gimmick 5): his dump at 16:10:10 lists the lift onward (`01eve_02` "Ride", 12 metres south) as reachable, the tiles gone. **Verified:** 0.0.100's flat boxes and 0.0.99's 52F table, "Next step" mode, the nearer-exit choice on 52F. **His requests:** the "Walk interrupted by an event." after every tile distracted him (three times; at the exit "Auto-walk stopped.", the view changed to first person); and a line at the end, in his words, "Puzzle successfully completed, reached other side." **Built:** `TimePuzzle.StepTaken(name)`: the step the game has just fired; `AutoWalk.Stop` leaves its stop line out when the walk's target was the next step and that tile fired (logged "Puzzle step X taken: ... not said"; a movement key pressed still says "Auto-walk stopped."); when flag 1614 goes off with every step taken (or the area changes then), `TimePuzzle.DoneWords` is said once the player has control (`FieldState.IsExploring`, within 15 s), held 3 s against key presses (`SpeechManager.SayHeld`); "Will you go back?" Yes ends it with steps left and says nothing. **To test (77F left, or any floor with a table):** the tile walks end silently, the game's "Time passed quickly" follows; after the exit "Puzzle successfully completed, reached other side."

**22 Oct 2026: 0.0.110 (built and pushed; log only): step 1 done, step 2 ready for the falling minigame.** Francesco's go-ahead (steps 1 and 2; never the executable). **Step 1, read offline from `GameAssembly.dll`:** the player's input is `pipeSection.ActionKey`: four `DDS3_PADCHECK_PRESS` checks, U and D (6, 7) and L and R (4, 5), and one `DDS3_PADCHECK_TRIG` of RD (3): the same game check `PadInput` already presses for walking, so the mod can steer through the game's own input. `pipeSection.MyHitAction`: touching an object of id 5, 6 or 9 adds macca (`pipeObject.pipeMyMakaAdd`, `pipeMakaAnim.makaAdd`); ids 1 and 2 put the player in a damage state that loses a share of the macca (`pipeMyMakaSubRate`) and scatters dropped coins and boxes (`objAddDMaka`, `objAddDRBOX`): harm costs macca, not HP; id 10 is tested first (unknown). The player is `ObjWork[pipeSection.myIndex]`; its place in the tube is `lrRad` and `udRad` (angles). `MyTask` and `MyAction` animate; `pipeMain.MainPipe` runs it. **Step 2:** `Capture/PipeProbe.cs` (tick "pipe probe"): in field 999 every 100 ms one `[Pipe]` line: `pipeFlow`, `myIndex`, `lrRad`, `udRad`, `myMaka`, `playerDAMAGE`, `myStatus`, `rBoxNum`, the directions really held (`PadInput.Real`), and every enabled object (`index:id/action pos vpos w status`). Says and changes nothing. **For Francesco:** one jump into a hole (any Kalpa), playing it normally, moving left and right and up and down a little; then the log tells the coordinates, ids and speeds for step 3 (the automation).

**22 Oct 2026, 20:55 session: the falling minigame studied (0.0.110's probe, 372 samples, 20:56:03 to 20:56:42; 285 macca, hit once).** **The player** is `ObjWork[149]`, id 128 (255 when it ends); its slot is the `pipeSection` static at 0x90 (the proxy's `Hitcount`: read by `chHitObj` as the player's index). Its `pos` is a point on the screen plane (x about -500 to 500, y about -200 to 360), `vpos` its velocity: R and L push `vpos.x` (+24 to +34, -12 to -34 per step), crossing the width in about 1 s; U and D change y (U lowers y: 234 to 189 at 114.09 s; D raises it). `lrRad` and `udRad` are the turn speeds (±9.5, ±20), back to 0 when keys are released; `myIndex` is the tube section reached (0 to 27), not the player. **Objects** share the plane: coins id 5 in rows and arcs out to radius 540 (a coin being taken: action 100, status 1, `vpos` its start); ids 1 (w 150) and 2 (w 80, status 0x4000 at times) the harmful ones (the hit at 95.89 s: `playerDAMAGE` 1, player action 2); id 10 (at 127, 127) and id 11 (w 1) seen rarely, unknown. Coins appear about 2 s before they are reached (first coins listed at 91.56 s, the first macca at 93.69 s with the player at x -150 by coins at -120). **Depth:** `chHitObj` compares `abs(obj.offset - player.offset)` (scaled) with the two sizes `w` plus a constant, then the plane distance: `offset` (0x10) is depth; the next objects to arrive are those whose offset is nearest the player's. **Input:** `PadInput.AfterPress` acts only inside `fldPlayerCalc` (`FieldState.InPlayerCalc`), so its held buttons do not reach the minigame yet: step 3 needs a gate set by a prefix on `pipeSection.ActionKey` (cleared by its postfix). **Step 3 design (for Francesco):** in field 999, each frame: the objects ahead by depth; avoid ids 1 and 2 whose plane distance at arrival would touch; else steer to the nearest-depth coin (ids 5, 6, 9, not already taken); hold L/R and U/D through `PadInput` with the velocity's lead; his own direction keys take over at once (the mod lets go while any is held, and a moment after).

F7 is now the only switch for routes: off, no point says "via N other areas" and Home stays in the area (`Toggle` already cancels a running journey; the Pages list is rebuilt on every Page Up, so the change is heard on the next press). **To test (a Kalpa, F7 on, then off):** with F7 on, only points reachable through doors already walked say "via N other areas"; plain doors of rooms not reached are not listed; with F7 off, no "via" at all; doors still say "Door to area N".

**23 Oct 2026: 0.0.112 (built and pushed, not yet tested): the falling minigame's autopilot (step 3; Francesco: on by default, F4 switches it off and on while falling, no line at the start).** Read offline first: `pipeSection.chHitObj` (RVA 0x2267EE0) touches an object when `abs(o.offset - p.offset) * 40` is within `o.w + p.w + len` and the plane distance of `pos` within `(int)(o.w + p.w + len)`, len 0 in `MyHitAction`'s main loop: so `offset` is depth and `pos` a point on one plane shared by the player and the objects; the static at 0x90 is the loop index, not the player (the player is still slot 149, id 128). **Built:** `Systems/Navigation/PipePilot.cs`: a prefix on `pipeSection.ActionKey` (the minigame's key read) holds L, R, U, D through `PadInput` (`PadInput.InPipeKey` opens its postfix gate only while ActionKey runs; the postfix clears it). Each frame: the objects whose depth gap closes (closing speed from the gap when first seen; the median for new ones), with the frames until they reach the player's depth zone `(w + 50) / 40`; target the soonest coin (ids 5, 6, 9, not action 100) reachable in time (28 units a frame) whose spot no harmful object (ids 1, 2) crosses at about the same time; a harmful object arriving within 25 frames near the player or the target pushes the target away from it; the target kept within radius 470; each axis held toward the speed that stops at the target (v² / 2a, a 6). R raises x, L lowers it, D raises y, U lowers it (the 20:55 probe run). Any real direction key lets go at once; the pilot waits 600 ms after the last. F4 in field 999: "Autopilot off." / "Autopilot on." (the state kept until the game closes; on at launch). The probe now also logs each object's depth (`d`), the player's (`me`) and what the pilot does (`pilot coin 137`, `away from 140`, `hold`, `manual`, `off`); target changes logged as `[Pipe] Autopilot: ...`. Untuned guesses to check in the log: the closing speed (ints, so coarse), the acceleration, whether ids 2 with status 0x4000 harm, whether the rim radius is right. **To test:** jump into a hole and do nothing: the macca at the end should be higher than the 95 and 285 of earlier runs, with few or no hits; press F4 once while falling ("Autopilot off."), again ("Autopilot on."); hold a direction key: it should be yours at once.

**23 Oct 2026: 0.0.113 (built and pushed, not yet tested): a performance and stability pass, no behaviour changed (Francesco asked for one).** Reviewed: every per-frame update (`ModMain.Updates`, the field-update prefixes, the TMP hook), the static collections (memory), the error handling, the speech thread and file writes, and the 20 archived session logs (17 to 23 Oct: no `[Update]` failure, no exception, every patch applied, grid builds at most 24 ms, route plans 43 ms). Found healthy: collections keyed by UI path or game content, the per-component ones cleared; the TMP classification cache capped; speech queue capped; the readers polled on timers; collision and NPC switches polled. **Changed:** (1) `MenuReader.FollowGameCursor` ran on every frame for the party pick, closed or not, and sorted the slots into a new list each time; the rows are now built only once the game draws the pick (`StockCursor.PartyPickSlot` takes them lazily, after its draw-flag checks), so the same answer with no per-frame list. (2) `MenuReader.Tick` logged every failure of a menu, and the game cursor is followed every frame: now the first five per menu. (3) `NavProbe.WatchChanges` made two closures on every frame: static delegates now (`_watchField`). (4) `AreaRoutes.Tick` built a string key on every frame while exploring: a number tuple now. (5) `PipePilot.Tick` closes `PadInput.InPipeKey` every frame, as `FieldState.Tick` does for `InPlayerCalc`, so an exception inside the game's `ActionKey` can never leave held keys reaching other key reads. Left as they are (measured small, or a change of behaviour risked): per-frame name reads in `TimePuzzle` and `PlaneShift` (their own fields only), small strings in the probe line. **To test:** play as usual; nothing should sound different (party picks in camp, items and skills, the routes, the minigame).

The walking map was right; the room was sealed. **Proposal (Francesco to choose):** (1) routes keep out of the volumes of story events not yet played when another way exists (as warps are), so Home finds the path that triggers no soul; (2) when nothing else can be reached, the reset warps are listed and said ("Warp, back to the start" or the game's own words), not an empty list; (3) a Shift+End way on for this room once the soul-free path is confirmed in game.

**27 Oct 2026: 0.0.120 (built and pushed, not yet tested): routes cross as few unplayed story events as they can; the reset warps listed when nothing else can be reached (Francesco chose proposals 1 and 2).** **1. Story events in routes:** `PointsOfInterest.Build` hands the walking map the volumes of the story events it lists (not yet played: a played or switched-off scene is left out) as soft zones (`WalkGrid.SetSoftZones`, applied lazily per grid build, `ApplySoft`, logged "N story event volumes avoided by routes where another way exists"); `PlanAllowing` adds `SoftCost` (2000, more than any detour) for entering one other than the target (`_allow`), so a route crosses the fewest story events, none where a way round exists; floods and regions are unchanged, so the list still shows what lies behind a soul. Logged "The route crosses N story events: no way round". First built as all-or-nothing avoidance and changed after the offline check: in the soul room no route avoids every soul. **Offline check** (`tools/navregions.pl`, new `NAVBLOCK="x0 z0 x1 z1;..."`: blocked rectangles, as warp volumes are; dump 13:39:44 at the start, every barrier down, the warps' and souls' volumes plus 45 blocked as chosen; barriers switched on by editing the dump's `switches:` line): from the start no soul-free route reaches the north door `01d_02`; crossing soul 05 (unavoidable, its barrier `hoji_05` lies behind, south) and then the west (02) or east (03) soul reaches it with those barriers up, and from that side alcove (-1200 or 2000, 3000) the door and the item box are reached crossing no other soul; crossing 05 then the centre soul (01) seals it (Francesco's session crossed 05, 01, 03, 02). The planner's choice from the start: 2 souls by a side corridor against 3 by the centre (01, 04). **2. The reset warps:** a touch warp is marked walked to only by the warp routes, and those run only where some warp's arrival is known; this room's two (`01d_03`, `01d_04`, "01pos_01 not found") never were, so a sealed room listed nothing. `PointsOfInterest.ListResetWarps`: when nothing is listed, every touch warp whose ring (`WarpMap.TargetRegions`) holds the player's region is listed ("Warp"), logged. **To test (Amala Network 2, area 5, from the start, after a reset warp if the room is sealed):** Page Up to the north door, Home: the soul by the start speaks (its barrier closes behind you), Home again: the walk takes a side corridor (west or east), that soul speaks, Home again: on to the door, no other soul; the log says "The route crosses 1 story event" at each soul. If a room is sealed: Page Up lists the warps.

**27 Oct 2026: 0.0.122 (built and pushed, not yet tested): the way-on tone (Francesco's design).** A short, unique tone tells that an area has a Shift+End way on: once, when the player first has control in such an area after a save is loaded or the game continued (the title screen and a load clear what was heard: `ExploredDoors.ClearAll` calls `WayOn.ForgetHeard`), and on first entering each such area; never again in that area until the next load (a battle and back plays nothing). Not high (Francesco finds high tones irritating) and unlike the wall thud: `Earcons.WayOn`, two soft, rounded notes rising a fourth, D4 then G4 (294 and 392 Hz) with a quarter of the octave for warmth, 12 ms attack, a bell-like decay, 450 ms; synthesised in memory like the thud (`Earcons.Play`, one `Wav` writer for both). `WayOn.Tick` (update loop), logged "has a way on: tone"; config `WayOnSound` (on). **To test:** continue in the Amala Network 2 soul room or any Second Kalpa area: the tone once when you have control; walk into another Second Kalpa area: the tone once there; back into an area already heard: nothing; an area without data (the Network's other areas): nothing.
